# Play-mode bug log

Every bug the user reported while playtesting `viewer/play.html`, in order, with its status.
Baseline for regressions: re-check the fixed ones when touching the same code.
Status: **fixed** (commit), **open**, **as original** (the exe does the same), **wontfix**, **ours: exe bug fixed**
(the exe does the same, but we deviate on purpose because the rule hurts play; the decision log 2026-10-30; switchable
in data/fixes.json where cheap, `"fixed": false` = the exe's rule for oracle comparisons).

closed or listed below.

| 363 | 10-31 | (1L-5) Earthquake cracks: the exe's pictures look like separate pieces where ours join into lines (oracle 1L5-quake m196 vs ours on tools/oracle_quake.py save +1000) | not a bug of ours (10-31): the comparison was wrong. The run poked the exe's camera to an odd ROW (281); the exe then draws the tiles of even x + y 30 px left of the even-row rule (cam (60 COL - 6870, 15 ROW - 30)) and those of odd x + y 30 px right of it: its screen rows shear 60 px apart, which breaks every crack line into pieces (rows only, the pictures are whole). Read back at dpr 1.25 (zoom 1/dpr) with our camera at the even-row rule - 30: of the plain tiles in m196, 153 of 153 with even x + y within 8 per channel and 146 of 153 with odd x + y not, those at + 60 px: 120 of 120; our crack picture at its tile with that per-parity offset equals the exe's pixels for 114 of 125 cracked tiles (> 90 % of the picture's opaque pixels within 8; the rest under taller pictures / dust). So our pictures (T_CRACKS 0x00636BE0, neighbours FUN_004A2FC0: rock 2 with piece 0x80, masks FUN_004A6BF0) are the exe's. A turn keeps rows even (rotation.md), real play never shows this. Fixed in the tools: tools/oracle_quake.py and tools/oracle_disaster.py poke even rows, tools/oracle_view_compare.py warns on an odd ROW (and lines up its even x + y tiles) |

| 455 | 11-04 | (user 11-04, 4th time this session, screenshot: REPLAY badge, slider short of the end) "my browser play keeps falling behind the live even though i dont touch anything ... it should just not get detached from live, make it robust" | fixed 11-04 (ours, play). Cause: a live tab drives the sim by asking for "playhead + margin" (frames.json want); after a hiccup (a stalled frame or answer > DRIVE_TIMEOUT 1 s, a loaded machine) the server ran alone, and the tab, asking for ticks the sim had passed, made the sim wait for it, so the lag stayed for good unless it exceeded the jump threshold (margin*4 + 60 = 76 at speed 30), which was above the live window (max(45, rate) + 2*REC = 75): a click from a lag in 0..76 could be refused ("not live") and build.js dropped the tab to replay. Now (play.js advance) a playhead behind its margin plays faster until it is back (3x the excess per second), the jump threshold is below the window (min(margin*4 + 60, window - 2*margin)), and postCmds (build.js; sendCommands and the pray button use it) has a refused live tab rejoin live at the server's tick and send once more, never detaching; a replay tab is still refused. (A first try, 9b214a8, sent no tick from live tabs; replaced.) Headless on a save copy: a 50-tick lag closes to 3 in 2 s; a live command from 5000 behind is accepted after the rejoin and the tab stays live; a replay tab's command is refused. tools/comms_check.mjs verify: frames / states / deltas 0 differ; 7 and 13 pixel ticks differ at the run's first ticks with "terrain lavaMap differs" (this save has lava; not compared against the old code, the worktree lacked generated files) |

After: 0 stalls, the set speed at 1x..16x. (2) The server's periodic cost: the building snapshot (every 30 ticks or 0.5 s, inside the driving poll, under the lock) took ~120 ms on the shared server's CPU (stall log: "lock 120 ms at advance" every 30 ticks = 2 s at 1x), mostly terrain_string's per-tile loop and per-field json.dumps in comms.delta: numpy terrain_string (15 -> 0.7 ms), direct compare of scalar fields, one JSONEncoder; record_state 37 -> 13.5 ms here.
